# Implementation questions and provisional decisions

Gaps found in the spec during implementation. Every row is provisional until the owner confirms it; confirmed rows may return to the spec as new D-n decisions, through a spec edit the owner approves ("Editing the spec" in `docs/agents/guidelines.md`). Code that relies on a row carries a `# Q-n` comment.

Status is one of: `provisional` (a new row), `confirmed` (the owner accepted the provisional decision), `changed (#n)` (the owner chose otherwise; the row's decision is updated and the code follows in issue #n: the row's own ticket when the owner answered during its build, otherwise an issue implemented on its own), or `in spec (D-n)` (returned to the spec). A row's other cells, and the wording of its decision cell, may be corrected, with the owner's approval, where they go stale while the decision holds; the Status stays as it is ("Where the prd files are written" in `docs/agents/guidelines.md`).

| Q-1 | §2.1 band table, §2.7 Organisation, U-2, U-3, U-9, U-21, U-28, U-31 | The public signatures of `ftgsxr-testkit`: §2.7 names its functions without full signatures, and the §3 rows need helpers it does not name (from #1: M-1 Core and I/O: the public signatures of `ftgsxr-testkit`) | `fixtures_dir() -> PathBuf` (verifies `SHA256SUMS` once per process, panics naming the first mismatch); `probe(name) -> (Model, RenderRequest)`; `reference_render(name) -> Vec<(String, RenderImage)>`; `Metrics { psnr, share, alpha }` (f64) and `metrics(a, b)` (E15, f64, `psnr` infinite at zero MSE); `random_model(seed, n, degree) -> Model` (valid, deterministic, a few persistent rows); `real_sh(degree, dir) -> [f64; 16]` (independent of `ftgsxr_core::sh`); `SafetensorsBuilder` (`new`, `metadata`, `tensor(name, dtype, shape, bytes)`, `pad_header`, `misalign_data`, `build`); `exchange_bytes(&Model) -> Vec<u8>`; `serve_fixtures() -> LocalServer` with `url`, `url_of`, `requests() -> Vec<RequestRecord>`, `truncate_next`, ETag/304 and Range/206; the listing choice as Q-6; `gpu_context` added in M-2 | the rows need these helpers; keeping them in the dev crate keeps core and io to §2.1's API | helpers private to each test crate; a §2 edit listing them | every row above compiles against them; U-9's `real_sh` disagreeing with `sh_basis` exposes either side | confirmed |
| Q-2 | E8, U-13, §2.1 `ftgsxr_core::covariance` | E8's CPU form: U-13 is a `cpu` test but §2.1 gives no CPU sort-key function (from #1: M-1 Core and I/O: E8's CPU form for U-13) | `pub fn sort_key(depth: f32) -> Option<u32>` in `ftgsxr_core::covariance` (k̄ = 2³² − 1 − bits(z) for finite z > 0, else `None`) and `pub fn sort_key16(key: u32) -> u16` (the top 16 bits) | the key is a function of E6's depth, which `covariance` mirrors; the GPU sort needs a CPU oracle | a new core module; U-13 only in the `gpu` lane | U-13; R-4 and R-5 in M-2 | confirmed |
| Q-3 | §1.11 scene files, U-30, §2.1 `ftgsxr_io::scene::{read_scene, write_scene}` | Where scene-file paths are made relative: the two functions receive no model directory (from #1: M-1 Core and I/O: where scene-file paths are made relative) | `read_scene` and `write_scene` store and return each `SourceSpec.location` as given; `ftgsxr_app::persist` (M-4) relativizes against `AppPaths.models` before writing and resolves after reading; in M-1, U-30 pins that relative and absolute locations round-trip unchanged. The rule (owner, #24): only a local filesystem path lexically under `AppPaths.models` (after normalizing `.` and `..`, without resolving symlinks or touching the disk) is written relative to it; URLs, Android `content://` URIs and paths outside `models` are written as given; on reading, a relative local location is joined onto `models`; each model's `audio` field follows the same rule | keeps `scene` a pure codec with §2.1's signatures; only the app knows the directory; relative to `models` keeps a scene portable between desktop and Quest | a `models_dir` argument (a §2 change); relative to the scene file's directory (scenes live in the config directory and models in the data directory, §1.11, so every path would climb out with `../`) | U-30 in M-1 (round trip as given); in M-4, `persist`'s tests (U-30's persistence part or I-9) must check that a model under `models` is written relative and resolves back to the same absolute path, and that a path outside `models`, a URL and a relative `audio` path follow the rule: I-9's §3 text alone does not cover it | confirmed |
| Q-4 | §1.5 download cache, §1.11 cache entries, U-28, §2.1 `HttpClient::prune` | How `prune` knows which cached files are loaded: it takes nothing that says so (from #1: M-1 Core and I/O: how `prune` knows which cached files are loaded) | a public-API addition `pub fn set_in_use(&self, paths: &[PathBuf])` on `HttpClient`, replacing the set of cached files `prune` must keep (interior mutability); the app (M-4) calls it whenever the set of loaded models changes, not only before pruning, and is the only caller of `prune`, which it runs only where no path `download` returned is still waiting to be registered or read | `prune` keeps §2.1's signature; the client stays unaware of instances | `prune(&self, keep: &[PathBuf])`; per-file pin and unpin; a lock file | U-28's prune clause; M-4's library check | confirmed |

| Q-7 | §2.7 Skeleton test, U-35, CLAUDE.md (SPDX headers) | Which files are "every source file of the workspace" that U-35 checks: neither says which extensions are source, whether the agent tooling under `.claude/` and the committed data fixtures count, or where a script's interpreter line goes (from #2: Workspace skeleton) | Source files are those with the extensions CLAUDE.md gives headers for: `//` in `.rs`, `.wgsl`, `.wesl`, `.kt`, `.kts`, `.gradle`; `#` in `.toml`, `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.sh`, `.py`; the header is the first line, or the second after a `#!` line. The walk starts at the workspace root and skips `.git`, `target`, `__pycache__`, `.venv`, `.gradle` and `build` directories anywhere, and `.claude/worktrees/`, `.scratch/` and `tests/fixtures/` at the root. The owner (#28): the scripts under `.claude/` are source and carry headers like any other, so the walk enters `.claude/` and skips only `.claude/worktrees/`, where each branch's worktree is a whole checkout | CLAUDE.md asks a header of every source file, the skills' scripts included (`setup-skills/scripts/create-labels.sh` gained its own on `workflow-spdx-headers`); worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` hold other branches' files, which a run from the main checkout must not judge; `tests/fixtures/` holds data in the documented formats (sample `settings.toml` files among them), checksummed, whose bytes a header would change | skip all of `.claude/` (the provisional decision); check every tracked file with `git ls-files`; exempt fixtures by extension only | a source file added under a skipped directory without a header passes unseen; a TOML or Python file elsewhere without one fails U-35; a script under `.claude/skills/` without one fails it | changed (#28) |
| Q-8 | §1.2 reader rules (`render_conventions`), §1.15 E-FILE-VALUE and E-JSON, D-39, U-2, §2.1 `RenderConventions::from_json`, `Model::validate` | The rules say only that a present block must be a JSON object and its `background` three numbers in \[0, 1\]; they give no code for a block that is not a JSON object (U-2 expects "its E-FILE code"), and say nothing of an entry the player honors (D-39) that is present with the wrong type (`"alpha_max": "0.999"`), nor of `transmittance_stop` given as `null` (from #4: `ftgsxr_core::types`) | A block that is not valid JSON or not a JSON object is `CoreError::Value` (E-FILE-VALUE); a numeric entry present with a value that is not a number finite in float32, or a string entry (`screen_extent_rule`, `rasterize_mode`) that is not a string, is `CoreError::Value`; an absent entry takes openftgs's value; `transmittance_stop: null` reads as `None` and `to_json` writes `None` as `null`; numbers are not range-checked beyond the background. `Model::validate` checks the same: the conventions' numbers finite and the background in \[0, 1\], as its last rule | E-FILE-VALUE's condition names "a metadata number that does not parse" and "a background outside \[0, 1\]"; E-JSON's names player files and requests only; since D-39 makes the numbers parameterize E3, E6 and E7, a non-number cannot be honored | the default with a warning, as openftgs's readers ignore every entry but the background (accepts more files); E-JSON or E-FILE-FORMAT for a non-object block; range checks on `alpha_max`, `near`, `far` | U-2's "render\_conventions that is not an object" clause (the loader ticket); `u01_bad_blocks_are_value_errors`; a file of another producer with a string-typed number is refused rather than rendered with openftgs's constant | confirmed |
| Q-9 | E10 (B\_m), §2.1 `Model::bounds(samples)` | "the box over μx(τ) … of every primitive with opacity > α\_skip, expanded by 3·max\_i max(scale\_i)": whether i in the margin ranges over the same primitives or over all rows, and what the box is when no primitive has opacity > α\_skip (from #4: `ftgsxr_core::types`) | The margin's maximum is over the same primitives as the box (opacity > the model's `alpha_skip`); when no primitive passes, the box and the margin are over every row; `samples` = 0 samples τ₀ alone, as a single timestamp does. μx(τ) is E1 computed in float64 and rounded to float32 (a private helper `types::model::moved_position_f64` the `motion` module may reuse) | the plain reading of the sentence; an invisible model still needs a finite box for §1.5's fit and E10's ray test, and the rows that exist are the only data | the margin over all rows (a faint huge primitive would inflate the box); an empty or degenerate box at the model's mean; refusing such a model | `types_api.rs` `bounds_*`; U-15 and U-18 on hand-built boxes do not reach the difference; a model with one large faint primitive would show a larger fit size under the alternative | in spec (D-42) |
| Q-10 | §3.2 R-2, R-19, §2.7 Fixtures, U-6 | R-2 and R-19 name the player's additional probes but give no primitives or sweeps, and §3.2's "32 items per probe" leaves what varies over the items open; U-6 names "a probe" at "τ" without choosing them (from #3: Fixtures) | `tools/fixtures/make_probes.py`, all on §3.2's 64 × 48 camera of focal 60 at (0, −4, 0), τ₀ = 0, τ₁ = 1.9, 32 items: `R2_overlap_deg3` two overlapping degree-3 primitives (anisotropic, rotated, random higher bands of scale 0.12, one moving with duration 0.9), on P5's 360° ring while τ runs over the span; `R2_white_background` one primitive (duration 0.5) of a model whose `render_conventions` background is white, request background black, over τ; `R2_single_timestamp` three degree-1 primitives with τ₀ = τ₁ = 0.95 and durations in the unit 1, at τ from −1 to 3; `R2_epoch_time` P2's moving primitive with duration 0.5 and τ₀ = 10⁹ s, over the span; `R19_transmittance` a near primitive of opacity 0.95 (depth 3.5, scale 0.12) over a far one of opacity 1 − 10⁻⁷ (α = α\_max, depth 4, scale 0.1), centred on a pixel centre at item 0, the principal point then moved by sub-pixel steps; U-6's snapshot is `R2_overlap_deg3` at τ = 1.3 | each probe isolates the clause its row names, in the L1 probes' style (one variable swept); the overlap probe combines motion, rotation and degree 3, so one PLY snapshot covers U-6's every clause; owner answer (2026-10-29, #31): confirmed. Its two changes needed none: `R2_single_timestamp` keeps zero velocities, since openftgs D-71's `validate_exchange` refuses a nonzero velocity (or a time center other than τ₀) when τ₀ = τ₁, so no valid file can carry the motion D-9 suppresses; `R2_white_background`'s request already states its black background, since the harness's `write_request` always writes `background` | other sweeps (time only, view only); a PLY of P2 (no SH bands) | R-2 and R-19 in M-2; U-6 (a snapshot without motion or bands would let a reordering or a time error pass) | confirmed |
| Q-11 | §3.2 R-2 ("a random model of N = 50,000 at 640 × 480 and three times"), §2.7 Fixtures, D-29 | At R-2's size the random probe alone commits about 19 MB (4.2 MB of model at degree 0, 14.7 MB of float32 renders); ticket #3 asks for it kept small, choosing degree and size (from #3: Fixtures) | `R2_random`: the harness's `random_exchange` (seed 50) with N = 10,000 at degree 0, rendered at 320 × 240 (focal 300, the probes' eye) at τ = 0.3, 0.95 and 1.6: 0.8 MB of model and 3.7 MB of renders (about 3.7 MB compressed together) | keeps the probe's purpose (many overlapping primitives of random shape, opacity and time, sorted per pixel) at a quarter of the pixels and a fifth of the rows | R-2's full size committed (about 19 MB more); the full size produced uncommitted into `$OPENFTGS_DATASETS/xr-fixtures/` by M-2's `train_tiny.sh` for the `ds` lane | R-2 in M-2 (a sort or overflow fault that appears only above 10,000 rows or at 640 × 480 would pass it; R-4's 10⁶ keys, R-5's 10⁴ primitives and R-6's overflow rows cover those scales) | confirmed |
| Q-12 | §3.2 R-7, R-8, §2.7 Fixtures (the pre-posed merged model), E5, E4 | The rows give the instances (P7's two primitives; one degree-3 instance rotated 90° about y and scaled 2) but not their poses, the merged model's construction, R-8's request or how a test learns the poses (from #3: Fixtures) | `tools/fixtures/merge_posed.py`: R-7's instances `merged/R7_instance_a` (P7's row 0, translation (0.04, 0, −0.03), 30° about x, scale 1) and `R7_instance_b` (row 1, translation (−0.03, 0, 0.02), 20° about z, scale 1.2), rendered at eight times from 0 to 1.9 (none within 0.02 of the depth tie); R-8's instance `probes/R2_overlap_deg3` rotated 90° about y (the model frame's y, the requests' world frame), scale 2, no translation, on that probe's 32-item ring; the merged models bake each pose in: position′ = t + sRp, velocity′ = sRv, scale′ = s·scale, rotation′ = q ⊗ rotation (canonical sign), times and opacity unchanged, and SH coefficients rotated so the colour along a world direction d is the instance's along R⁻¹d (one matrix over all bands, fitted by least squares on openftgs's `sh_basis`; exact, since each band is closed under rotation); the poses are in `merged/R7_poses.json` and `R8_poses.json` (`translation`, `rotation` as (x, y, z, w) like §1.11's `poses.json`, `scale`) | the reference render can only come from one openftgs model, so the poses must be baked in, the SH included or R-8 would compare different colours at degree 3; R-7's depth tie stays at τ = 0.95, 0.136 from the nearest of its eight times, with the two primitives within about 4 px on screen. Instance a's 30° rotation has no visible effect (an isotropic, static, degree-0 primitive at the origin), so R-7 poses instance a by its translation only and R-8 carries the rotation test | identity poses for R-7 (would not test posing); Wigner matrices written out by hand | R-7, R-8 in M-2 (a wrong SH rotation or quaternion order fails R-8 at degree 3) | confirmed |
| Q-13 | §2.7 Fixtures ("`ftgs info`'s reply"), §1.7 (`info`, openftgs §3.3's adapter contract) | openftgs 0.1.0 has no `ftgs info` command; the reply exists only as `ftgs-adapter info`, whose global `--backend` flag chooses the rasterizer it reports (from #3: Fixtures) | the fixtures README records `ftgs-adapter --backend reference info`, the reply for the backend that made the reference renders | it is the adapter reply §1.7 refers to; the default reply names gsplat, which made no fixture. | Q-14 | §1.11 Locations, §2.1 `AppPaths::linux`, PRD #1 ("Paths" and the added `AppPaths` test) | §1.11 puts screenshots under `$XDG_PICTURES_DIR/openftgs-xr/`, but `XDG_PICTURES_DIR` is an entry of the user-dirs file `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/user-dirs.dirs`, which `directories` reads (`UserDirs::picture_dir`) without ever reading an environment variable of that name, and which may be missing, with no default; the PRD's test sets it as an environment variable. Neither says what `linux` returns without a home directory, nor whether it creates the directories (from #16: `ftgsxr_io::paths` and `IoError`) | The screenshots root is the environment variable `XDG_PICTURES_DIR` when it is set to an absolute path, else the user-dirs file's entry (`UserDirs::picture_dir`), else `$HOME/Pictures`; `screenshots` is that root joined with `openftgs-xr`. The other five follow `directories`' `ProjectDirs` for the project path `openftgs-xr` (`$XDG_*_HOME` when absolute, else the XDG defaults under the home directory). `linux` returns `IoError::Io` (E-IO, kind `NotFound`) when no home directory is found, and only computes paths: it creates no directory (each writer creates what it writes into) | the variable honors the PRD's test and environments that export it; the user-dirs file is where desktops record it; `~/Pictures` is the usual name when a system has neither; computing without side effects keeps `linux` testable and lets the E-IO of a missing directory surface where a file is written | read the user-dirs file only (the test would write that file instead); fall back under the data directory (`$XDG_DATA_HOME/openftgs-xr/screenshots`); an error when no pictures directory is known; create the six directories in `linux` | `app_paths_linux.rs`: the variable, the user-dirs file and the fallback each give the screenshots location, and a relative variable is ignored | confirmed |
| Q-15 | §1.11 Locations (Quest), §2.1 `AppPaths::android(internal, external: Option<&Path>, cache)` | The signature allows no app-external files directory (Android returns none while shared storage is unavailable), but §1.11 places the models and screenshots only there (from #16: `ftgsxr_io::paths` and `IoError`) | Without an external directory, `models` and `screenshots` are `<internal>/models` and `<internal>/screenshots`; with one, `<external>/models` and `<external>/screenshots`. In both cases `config` is the internal directory itself, `scenes` is `<internal>/scenes`, `cache` is the cache directory itself and `logs` is `<cache>/logs`, as §1.11 lays out | the player keeps working with models it can still reach (downloads, picked documents), in a directory the system does not clear, at the cost of adb and MTP access until storage returns; owner answer (2026-10-29, #36): confirmed, with two notes for other rows: settings record the default model directory as the default, not as the absolute path `AppPaths` gives, so a run in fallback does not keep `<internal>/models` once external storage returns (Q-49, Q-51); the Android platform layer, which sees the missing directory, logs a warning and shows a toast (§1.13, §1.15) saying that models and screenshots stay in internal storage, out of adb and MTP reach, and are not moved when it returns | `models` and `screenshots` under the cache directory (the system may clear it); a panic or an empty path (the signature returns no `Result`, and changing it is a §2 change) | `app_paths_android.rs`: the clause without an external directory | confirmed |
| Q-16 | §2.1 `motion::temporal_window`, E3, D-39, U-8 | The doc comment gives the `None` condition as 255·opacity ≤ 1 while the signature takes the model's `alpha_skip` (D-39); it does not say in which precision α\_skip enters the window, nor whether `span` is T\_τ or E3's T′ (from #7: `ftgsxr_core::motion`) | `None` exactly when opacity ≤ `alpha_skip` (the two float32 values compared exactly), with or without `widened`, as E3's precondition opacity\_i > α\_skip reads; otherwise w is computed in float64 with α\_skip promoted exactly from float32 (for openftgs's files the float32 nearest 1/255, not the decimal 1/255); `span` is T\_τ = τ₁ − τ₀ and the function takes T′ = 1 when `span` is not positive, as E3 says for a single timestamp | for openftgs's α\_skip, the float32 nearest 1/255, which lies just above 1/255, the two conditions agree on every float32 opacity but α\_skip itself, whose window would be 0 and which E3 calls never active; the cull pass tests σ\_eff against the same float32 α\_skip from the uniform block (D-39), so the window and the exact test share one bound; with T′ inside, a caller passes `TimeSpan`'s span as it is | 255·opacity ≤ 1 literally (ignores a producer's own α\_skip, D-39); α\_skip re-read as a float64 decimal (moves the bound by 1.5·10⁻⁸ relative, beyond U-8's 10⁻⁹); `span` as T′ chosen by the caller (the same numbers for every positive span) | `u08_motion_window.rs`: the closed form against σ\_eff < α\_skip agreeing within 10⁻⁹ of the bound; `None` at opacity = α\_skip and for 255·opacity ≤ 1; the widened margin 10⁻⁶ at `span` 0 | confirmed |
| Q-17 | E11 (two hands; revision 34's grab points), §1.5 Poses (fit, presets), §2.1 `ftgsxr_core::pose` | Inputs for which E11's and §1.5's formulas give no answer: hands that start at one point (‖b₀ − a₀‖ = 0 divides), a hand axis with no horizontal part (θ = atan2(0, 0)) or no length (free rotation), a head looking straight up or down (its yaw is undefined), a box with a zero longest side, and `from_camera_ups` with no cameras, a zero mean up or two presets equally near (from #11: `ftgsxr_core::pose`) | Below 1 µm a length counts as zero. A two-hand grab whose a₀ and b₀ start together (E11's grab points, or the hands' aim positions under its fallback) keeps the ratio 1; an axis with no horizontal part (upright) or no length (free rotation), at the start or now, gives no rotation, the scale and midpoint rules still applying. `fit_pose` takes the head's forward as the horizontal part of R(q)(0, 0, −1); when that is zero it takes the horizontal part of R(q)(0, 1, 0), the direction the top of the head points, negated when the head looks up; a longest side that is not positive and finite gives scale 1. `from_camera_ups` returns Y-up for no cameras or a mean shorter than 1 µm, and breaks ties in the order Y-up, Z-up, −Y-up | the spec's own defaults (Y-up, the scale the model already has) where it has one; no NaN reaches a pose; the head's top points where the face would when looking down. "No rotation" is relative to P₀, so a two-hand grab that has turned the model jumps back to its start yaw while the hands' axis is degenerate; tracking noise (millimetres) makes the 1 µm case practically unreachable, and this jump is accepted in core, whose `update(&self, …)` holds no state; `ftgsxr_app::manipulate` may hold the last pose while the hands are degenerate when A-3 is built | NaN or a panic; keeping the last valid θ (needs state the grab does not hold); refusing the fit | `u16_*` and `u18_*` exercise only regular inputs; a grab with stacked hands that jumps or produces NaN in A-3 would expose a wrong choice | confirmed |
| Q-18 | §1.5 Poses (fit), D-11, §2.1 `fit_pose` | Which rotation the fit gives the instance: §1.5 says the box is placed "after the orientation preset" and says nothing of the head's yaw or of the rotation the instance had (from #11: `ftgsxr_core::pose`) | The fitted pose's rotation is the preset's rotation followed by the yaw about stage +y that takes stage −z to the head's horizontal forward (Q-17), R\_y(ψ) · R\_preset with ψ = atan2(−f\_x, −f\_z); its scale is `size_m` over the fit box's longest side in model space (§1.5: B\_c by default, B\_m when `fit_to` is `whole`); its translation puts the posed box's center at the head's position plus `ahead_m` along that forward, at the height where the posed box's lowest corner lies on y = 0 | stage space's orientation is fixed when the guardian is set up, so a preset-only rotation would show the user an arbitrary side of the model, the automatic first-load fit included; turning by the head's yaw keeps the same model-space side (the one the preset takes to stage +z) toward the user wherever they stand, about vertical only, so the model stays upright; a head facing −z gives the preset's rotation alone. Keeping the instance's yaw is not possible: the signature does not take the instance's pose (owner, 2026-10-29, #39) | the preset's rotation alone (the provisional decision: the same stage-space pose wherever the user stands); keep the instance's yaw (a §2 signature change) | U-18's turned-head fit clause pins the yaw (the model's x along stage −z for a head yawed 90°); A-1 shows the orientation a first-loaded model appears in | changed (#39) |
| Q-19 | E9, §1.6 Clock, §2.1 `ftgsxr_core::clock::Clock` | The clock's behaviour where E9 and §1.6 give only "wraps (loop), reflects (ping-pong) or pauses at the end (once)" and §2.1's setters cannot refuse: the initial state, the exact end in loop, the speed's sign after a reflection, whether once honours the playback range, a T outside the range, and out-of-domain arguments to `seek`, `set_range` and `set_length` (from #10: `ftgsxr_core::clock`) | `Clock::new` starts at T = 0, speed 1, `Loop`, `Paused`, no range. The active interval is the range \[T\_a, T\_b\] in loop and ping-pong and \[0, T\_len\] in once (§1.6's parenthetical names only loop and ping-pong). `advance(dt)` does nothing while paused, when T\_len = 0 (E9's idle clock) or for a negative or non-finite dt (the direction comes from the speed alone); otherwise a T outside the active interval is first clamped into it, then T += speed·dt and the mode maps it back without iterating: loop wraps into the half-open \[T\_a, T\_b) by Euclidean remainder (reaching T\_b exactly gives T\_a; going backwards, below T\_a wraps towards T\_b); ping-pong folds by a triangle wave of period 2(T\_b − T\_a) and negates the speed once per odd number of reflections, so `speed()` is always the signed rate dT/dt; once stops at T\_len going forward or 0 going backwards, at or beyond it, and sets `Paused`. `seek` clamps to \[0, T\_len\] and ignores a non-finite value; `set_range` orders the pair and clamps it to \[0, T\_len\], and a non-finite bound or equal bounds after clamping (a degenerate range) clear the range; | Q-39 | §2.7 Organisation (`serve_fixtures() -> LocalServer`), Q-1, Q-6, §1.5 Sources and Download cache, D-31, U-28, U-31 | The fixture server's interface and behaviour: Q-1 names `url`, `url_of`, `requests`, `truncate_next`, ETag/304 and Range/206, Q-6 `serve_listing`, without signatures, record fields, routes or validator rules | `serve_fixtures() -> LocalServer`, a new tiny_http server on `127.0.0.1` and a free port per call, stopped on drop. `LocalServer::url(&self) -> String` is the root (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>/`); `url_of(&self, path: &str) -> String` the URL of `fixtures_dir()/path`, each segment percent-encoded; the listing base is `url_of("listing/models/")`, where the committed listing bodies' relative paths resolve. `serve_listing(&self, kind: ServedListing)`, `ServedListing { Index, NginxJson, Html }` (Q-6), default `Index`: under `Index`, `<base>index.json` answers `listing/index.json` and the base itself 404; under `NginxJson` or `Html` the base answers `listing/nginx.json` (`application/json`) or `listing/apache.html` (`text/html`) and `<base>index.json` 404. `requests(&self) -> Vec<RequestRecord>`, every request in arrival order, recorded before its response is written: `RequestRecord { method: String, path: String /* the request target as sent */, headers: Vec<(String, String)>, status: u16, body_bytes: u64 /* body bytes sent */ }` with `header(&self, name) -> Option<&str>` (case-insensitive). `truncate_next(&self, keep: u64)`: Q-40. Every other path serves the file under `fixtures_dir()` (percent-decoded, query ignored, `.` and `..` segments refused); a directory or a missing file is 404; methods other than GET and HEAD are 405. Each file and body has a strong ETag, the quoted lowercase hex SHA-256 of its bytes, and the fixed `Last-Modified: Thu, 29 Oct 2026 12:00:00 GMT` (the listing bodies' mtime); `If-None-Match` (weak comparison, `*` matches) answers 304 when it matches, else, without `If-None-Match`, an `If-Modified-Since` equal to that date string answers 304. A single `Range: bytes=a-b`, `a-` or `-n` answers 206 with `Content-Range`, or 416 with `bytes */<size>` when unsatisfiable; several ranges or a malformed header are ignored (200); `If-Range` with another ETag or date also gives 200. `Accept-Ranges: bytes` on 200 and 206; `Content-Type` by extension (`.json`, `.html`, `.txt`, else `application/octet-stream`) | each U-28 and U-31 clause can be driven and observed through these: the ETag and the date for the validators, `If-Range` for the "resume when the validators still match, else restart" of §1.5, the records' `Range` header and `body_bytes` for the header-only reads, the request order for D-31's detection order; a 404 base (another directory) gives the E-NET-LISTING case without a fourth variant. The exact-string `If-Modified-Since` match is the test server's simplification, enough because a client echoes the `Last-Modified` it stored; a server without validators or ranges (§1.5's "supports neither") is not offered, and would be a new row if a §3 row needed it (#60) | `url()` as the listing base; a generated listing for every directory; date comparison for `If-Modified-Since`; `serve_listing` also answering at the base under `Index` | U-28's 304, resume and header-only clauses and U-31's three flavors fail against the server if a rule above is wrong; the server's own unit tests drive each rule over a raw socket | confirmed |
| Q-40 | Q-1 (`truncate_next`), §1.5 Download cache ("a partial download is resumed with a `Range` request"), U-28 ("a resume after a truncated download") | How the server truncates a response: tiny_http cannot close a kept-alive connection from a handler (source log 43), so a body cut short of its `Content-Length` leaves the client waiting until its own timeout (45) | `truncate_next(keep)` arms one truncation; the next response with a body (a 200 or 206 to a GET) is written raw with `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` instead of `Content-Length`, its status, ETag, `Last-Modified` and (for 206) `Content-Range` unchanged, sends the first `keep` bytes of its body (all of it when shorter) as one chunk, pauses 50 ms so the client usually reads them first, then sends an invalid chunk-size line, so the client's body read fails at once; under heavy load the client can receive fewer than `keep` bytes before the failure (#398); the record's `body_bytes` is the bytes sent | the only immediate failure tiny_http allows; ureq reports it as a protocol error, usually after delivering the bytes (45), as a dropped connection would give an I/O error, so a client that keeps what it received and resumes with `Range` passes either way. The 50 ms pause makes the loss rare but does not prevent it. Without it, the invalid line reaches ureq with the last bytes and the read fails before delivering them all: 20 runs of U-28 without the pause all resumed at `bytes=290816-` for a `keep` of 300 000, the file still whole (#61). With it, 30 runs under 48 busy loops on 24 CPUs passed (#61), and so did 35 runs under 24 or 48 (#398). But under a load average near 50 from other builds, plus 24 busy loops, 15 of 20 runs lost the last read's bytes, and 10 of 20 still did with a 500 ms pause (#398). So U-28 checks the resume against the bytes the partial holds, not against `keep`. The truncated response carries no `Content-Length`, so U-28 does not reach the client's "the body ended early" check (a clean close before `Content-Length`), which no test exercises (#61) | a short body under the full `Content-Length` and an idle connection (needs a client read timeout, slow tests); a raw `TcpListener` beside tiny_http (§2.3 names tiny_http) | U-28's resume clause: a client that discards a partial download on an error, or a server whose truncation hangs, fails it | confirmed |

A `GpuTimes` field is 0 when its pass wrote no timestamp that frame or the device lacks `TIMESTAMP_QUERY`; `total` runs from the first timestamp written that frame to the last one written, and is 0 when none was written (Q-115). A result is `{setup: {…}, measure, value, unit}`; a budget `{id, setup, measure, rule, limit, value, pass}` with `rule` one of `at_least`, `at_most`, `within_1_percent`; the top level adds `profile` (`release` or `debug`), `baseline` (the path compared against, or null) and `notes`. `machine`: `gpu` the adapter's name, `driver` the first line of `/proc/driver/nvidia/version` (else `unknown`), `os` `/etc/os-release`'s `PRETTY_NAME` and the kernel release, `cpu` `/proc/cpuinfo`'s first `model name`, `threads` `available_parallelism`; `versions`: the crate's version and the pinned bevy and wgpu versions (constants a `cpu` test checks against `Cargo.lock`). The baselines are `$FTGSXR_BENCH_BASELINES/<NAME>.json` when the variable is set, else `<workspace>/benchmarks/baselines/<NAME>.json` (the path of the build); a missing file compares nothing, a malformed one is E-JSON (exit 2). Only budgets are compared (§3.5's "budgeted figure"), and only against a baseline of the same `profile` and `machine.gpu`: with another, nothing is compared and a line in `notes` and on standard error says which differs; `within_1_percent` budgets (B-6) are not compared, their own budget being the check. Each other budget is compared with the baseline's budget of the same id, setup and measure (objects compared as sets of members): worse by more than 10 % is `value` < 0.9 × baseline for `at_least`, `value` > 1.1 × baseline for `at_most`; each is one line in `regressions`, and the binary exits 3 when any is listed. A call over 1 ms during which the thread made no voluntary context switch, only involuntary ones (read from `/proc/thread-self/status` before and after the call), was preempted rather than waiting: it is reported and does not fail; where the counts can't be read, nothing is excused. Left `stick` = `move`; right `stick` = (`turn`, `push_pull`); `primary` = X (`fit`) on the left, A (`play_pause`) on the right; `secondary` = Y (`mode`) left, B (`reset_pose`) right; `menu` = `menu` (left only); `trigger` = `select`'s value, `squeeze` = `grab`'s; `pinch`, `pinch_middle` 0. A hand is `Some` while its `aim` action is active for that hand, `tracked` = the aim space's position and orientation tracked. Poses (scale 1) are the action spaces located in the primary reference space, then the tracking root's transform applied (the eyes' frame, the LOCAL fallback's 1.5 m included); `head` = the two located views' midpoint and the slerp of their orientations at 0.5, in the same frame. `XrHands` is written in PostUpdate after the views are located; `left_handed` = `[locomotion] left_handed` (Q-49, `Settings::default()` without `SettingsRes`) | D.2's bindings give both sticks in full (left by `move`, right by `turn` and `push_pull`), so M-5's left-handed swap needs no extra binding; an active pose action is OpenXR's "a device is bound and present"; one frame for the eyes, the rays and the head | bind `move` and `turn` on both hands; `None` only when untracked; poses in the raw reference space (off by 1.5 m from the eyes under LOCAL) | the added `xr` test (`xr_controllers_player_set_attaches_and_xr_hands_are_controllers`); I-16 in M-5 (the simple controller's aim to `Pointer`) | confirmed |

A step down takes the first knob of the ladder that can move, a step up the last. `Dynamic` foveation is left alone. e > 1.05 counts over and restarts under, e < 0.8 the reverse, e in [0.8, 1.05] restarts both; a step restarts the streak that fired, even when no knob could move; while `enabled` is false `govern` returns `None` and restarts both. The system counts a frame only when `SplatStats` changed and is `Some` (a new block), and writes `SplatTierState` only on a step. A device without timestamps gives `total` 0 (Q-81), so e = 0: the knobs only rise back to the tier's values | E16 counts frames of the GPU, and a block read again is not a new frame; releasing past the tier's value would raise quality above what the tier records (quest-low's 0.8); exact tenths keep U-22's "exact" | counting every app frame (the same GPU frame counted up to the stats' lag); a release ceiling of E16's 1.0 and 3 whatever the tier; stepping `Dynamic` as `High` | `u22_the_knobs_step_down_after_10_frames_over_and_up_after_120_under_in_order`, `u22_a_knob_is_released_only_to_the_tiers_own_value` (`crates/ftgsxr-app/tests/u22_governor.rs`); `i12_splat_tier_state_steps_in_order_from_injected_frame_times` (frames without a new block) | confirmed |

| Q-230 | §1.5 Library ("entries whose file changed (size, ETag or SHA-256) lose their cached information"), §1.5 Sources (HTTP), §2.1 `ftgsxr_io::http` (`HttpClient`), §2.1 `ModelSource::info`, §2 `LibraryEntry` | §1.5 compares ETags on refresh, but `Entry` and `LibraryEntry` (both §2 signatures) carry no ETag, none of the three listings gives one, `ModelSource::info` returns no response headers, and §2.1 names no call that returns a URL's validators (from #375: ftgsxr-io/app: a library refresh compares ETags) | `HttpClient::revalidate(&self, url: &Url, etag: Option<&str>) -> Result<Revalidation, IoError>` sends one `HEAD` of `url`, with `If-None-Match: <etag>` when given; `pub enum Revalidation { Unchanged, Current(Option<String>) }`: a 304 is `Unchanged`, a 2xx `Current` with the response's `ETag` (None when it sent none), any other status or a failure to reach the server E-NET-FETCH. Re-exported from `ftgsxr_io`. The app keeps each HTTP entry's ETag privately (beside the library, keyed by its source and location): the background task that reads an `Exchange` entry's header-only information first calls `revalidate(url, None)` and keeps the ETag it returns with the information read after it; a failed `HEAD` leaves the entry without an ETag. On refresh, an HTTP entry whose size, mtime and SHA-256 still match and that holds information and an ETag keeps them provisionally and is revalidated with that ETag in the background: `Unchanged` keeps them, and so does `Current` with the same ETag (a server that ignores `If-None-Match`); any other `Current` drops the information, identity and thumbnail and reads the header again (which takes the new ETag); an entry without an ETag is compared as before. A new refresh of a source drops its checks still running. The triage candidate had the header read keep its range response's own ETag; since `info`'s §2.1 signature returns no headers, the ETag comes from a `HEAD` sent just before the read instead | keeps `Entry`, `LibraryEntry` and `info` as §2 states them; one bodiless request per entry when its information is read, and one per unchanged entry per refresh (a changed entry's new read sends its own `HEAD`); the `HEAD` goes before the range reads, so a file that changes between them pairs an older ETag with newer information and the next refresh reads it again, never the reverse; the information stays on screen while the check runs. A server that does not answer `HEAD` leaves its entries without an ETag (I-3's test server answers 404, so I-3 does not exercise the check) | the header read's range response keeping its ETag (needs `info`'s signature changed, a §2.1 edit, or hidden per-URL state in the client); a conditional range request instead of `HEAD` (works where a server refuses `HEAD`, at a few body bytes each); an `etag` field on `Entry` (a §2.1 edit that no listing could fill); a spec edit replacing "ETag" with mtime | `revalidate` against `serve_fixtures`: no ETag gives the file's, its own ETag gives `Unchanged` with a 304, another gives `Current` with the file's; a refresh of unchanged HTTP entries sends a `HEAD` with `If-None-Match` answered 304 and no range read, and keeps the information; an entry whose server answers 200 to that `HEAD`, with no ETag or another one, loses its information and is read again; one whose server ignores `If-None-Match` and answers 200 with the same ETag keeps its information and is not read again | confirmed |

| Q-243 | §2.3 (wgpu-core and wgpu-hal 29.0.4 vendored "with one change each"; §2.1's dependencies of `ftgsxr-desktop`), D-30, §1.13 GPU validation, §3.2 R-16, §2.4 (presentation), Q-104 | §2.3 vendors wgpu only for D-30's flags fix, but R-16's zero validation messages can't hold for windowed `play` on wgpu 29.0.4: in the first frames, while pipelines compile, Bevy presents a surface texture no submitted command used, and wgpu presents the image in the `UNDEFINED` layout (`VUID-VkPresentInfoKHR-pImageIndices-01430`) and never waits on its acquire semaphore, which is still signaled when the image is acquired again (`VUID-vkAcquireNextImageKHR-semaphore-01286`); upstream, gfx-rs/wgpu#9213 is fixed only in wgpu 30.0.0 (#9361) and #9882 is open (from #401: play: windowed debug sessions report swapchain validation errors (VUID-VkPresentInfoKHR-pImageIndices-01430, vkAcquireNextImageKHR-semaphore-01286)) | a second change to the vendored wgpu-core 29.0.4, marked in `src/present.rs` with a tracker query in `src/track/texture.rs`: when `Surface::present` finds the acquired texture untouched since its acquire (no submission and no pending write used it), it clears it through the queue's pending writes and submits them first, so the submission waits on the acquire semaphore and moves the image to the present layout; a texture with pending writes not yet submitted is submitted the same way; a frame that rendered presents as before; a surface configured without `RENDER_ATTACHMENT` is not cleared, only submitted, so its image may still be presented in the `UNDEFINED` layout (harmless here: Bevy always configures `RENDER_ATTACHMENT`); the path runs on every backend, as wgpu 30's fix does. wgpu-hal is unchanged (the brief named both crates; only wgpu-core needed the change). Dropped once a wgpu release that Bevy's pin takes carries both fixes: gfx-rs/wgpu#9361 (in 30.0.0) and the fix of the open gfx-rs/wgpu#9882. Its test, `ftgsxr-desktop`'s `present_undrawn`, makes the window with winit and presents through wgpu: dev-dependencies `wgpu` (feature `vulkan`) and `winit` (`rwh_06`, `x11`, `wayland`), outside §2.1's row for the crate, as Q-104 was for serde\_json, and the workspace's `winit` entry with default features off, as Bevy takes winit, so the lock file gains no crate. Owner answer on #406, 2026-11-03: confirmed | the fault is upstream and reproduces with bare Bevy; no 29.x release fixes it; wgpu 30 needs Bevy 0.20, which bevy\_mod\_openxr 0.6.0 doesn't support (Q-139), and still has 01286; the vendored copies already exist (D-30); the submission path already waits on the acquire semaphore and transitions the surface textures it uses | upgrade Bevy and wgpu (blocked, and still 01286); `RenderPlugin::synchronous_pipeline_compilation` (leaves 1–2 errors); a spec edit letting R-16 tolerate dependency messages (hides real ones); drawing to the window every frame from the app (not guaranteed while Bevy's own pipelines compile); a fix in wgpu-hal's Vulkan present alone (it records no commands, so it can't clear the image) | R-16 for windowed `play`: the in-process `gpu_` test of a present without a draw, and #394's `gpu_` test | in spec (D-52) |
| Q-244 | §2.3 (the vendored crates: wgpu-core and wgpu-hal 29.0.4, D-30, D-52; bevy\_mod\_openxr, Q-139), §1.13 GPU validation, §3.2 R-16 (its scope and its "zero validation messages"), §1.7 Window ("One resizable window"), §2.4 (presentation) | R-16 covers R-1 to R-15, none of which resizes a window, but §1.7 makes the window resizable, and the owner wants resizes clean in debug builds too (decided on #409, 2026-11-03); the added resize tests widen what R-16 checks, which the spec doesn't yet say (R-16's scope, or a new R-n). And a resize of `play`'s window on X11 isn't clean: when the surface is `Outdated`, `bevy_render` 0.19.1 reconfigures it with its cached, old size, and `vkCreateSwapchainKHR` gets an extent outside the surface's current one (`VUID-VkSwapchainCreateInfoKHR-pNext-07781`, one per resize, at human speed too, the extent seconds out of date); the bug is still in Bevy 0.20.1 and on `main` (bevyengine/bevy#3451, open). wgpu-hal's messenger callback silences `07781` by its id, so Bevy's log and Q-105's in-process guard never show it; only the layer's own log does. §2.3 vendors no Bevy crate (from #409: play: resizing the window reports VUID-VkSwapchainCreateInfoKHR-pNext-07781 (swapchain extent outside the surface's)). Found in the implementation, against the brief's "the later size-changed reconfigure … configures at the same size": when the window is resized in quick succession (a drag, a burst 20 to 150 ms apart), the size-changed reconfigure of `create_surfaces` also passes a stale size, the extracted one, which the window system has already left. And a resize can land between Bevy's own query of the surface and wgpu-core's query inside `configure`, which then waits 0.1 to 5 ms for the GPU before `vkCreateSwapchainKHR`, so a fix inside Bevy leaves an occasional `07781` in bursts (1 or 2 messages in about a third to a half of the runs of a first 7-resize burst, 0 or 1 in five runs of the 6-resize burst test, and one message over six manual `play` bursts, three before the review fixes and three after; none for resizes seconds apart): the resize race that wgpu-hal's callback silences | Vendor `bevy_render` 0.19.1 at `vendor/bevy_render-0.19.1` through `[patch.crates-io]` with one change, in `src/view/window/mod.rs`, at both sites that reconfigure an existing window surface: when `get_current_texture` returns `Outdated` (`prepare_windows`, a public system, which gains a `Res<RenderAdapter>` parameter) and when the window's size or present mode changed (`create_surfaces`), the configuration takes the surface's fixed current extent, read through wgpu's `as_hal` on Vulkan (wgpu-hal's surface capabilities) as the last step before reconfiguring, as its width and height; with no fixed extent (Wayland, where any extent is valid), a zero extent, another backend, or a platform other than Linux and Android, as before. `Suboptimal` and the frames that reconfigure nothing are unchanged. The vendored copy keeps its license files; the change is named in NOTICE, in the workspace manifest's `[patch.crates-io]` comment and in the vendored file. Dropped once a Bevy release that the workspace takes fixes it. The race left after the change, a resize that lands between Bevy's query of the surface and wgpu-core's own query inside `configure`, is accepted: its `07781` in windowed `play` while the window is resized in quick succession is not fixed, as wgpu treats it (wgpu-hal's messenger silences it as a resize race; gfx-rs/wgpu#1958, #3095, #6927), and the resize test of a burst tolerates `07781` and no other message, while resizes seconds apart must leave none. Route approved by the owner, 2026-11-03 (#409); the size-changed site and the accepted race, owner answer during the build, 2026-11-03 (#409): "bevy fix and (b). Since it's accepted upstream, we will also accept it here" ("accepted upstream": wgpu silences the race's message; no upstream project has taken this fix) | the fault is upstream and in every Bevy version reachable; the app can't work around it, because wgpu's public capabilities don't report the current extent and the render world has no window-system access; the vendoring pattern already exists (Q-139, D-30, D-52); the layer has accepted the genuine race since 2022 (Vulkan-ValidationLayers#3833), so the stale size Bevy passes, not the race, causes the `07781` of every resize; wgpu won't fix it upstream (gfx-rs/wgpu#1958, #3095, #6927); a Bevy-side fix corrects the code that actually misuses the API, and could be proposed to Bevy; the race left can be closed only where the swapchain is created, inside wgpu, by a change wgpu would not take (below), so it would be carried with no drop condition | leave it (an invalid call that today's driver tolerates; the owner chose to fix it); on `Outdated`, skip the frame until the new size arrives (a window could stay blank if the surface goes out of date with no size change; bevyengine/bevy#12542 tried `continue` there and found small issues, such as bevy_egui's scale not updating with the window); clamp the extent inside wgpu-hal's `configure` (the swapchain images would no longer match the size wgpu-core and Bevy think they are); filter `07781` in R-16's layer guard, as wgpu-hal's callback does (D-52's rejected "R-16 tolerating dependency messages", it would hide the stale size of every resize, and it needs a change to R-16 or Q-105); upgrade Bevy (blocked, D-1, Q-139, and not fixed there); the `Outdated` reconfigure alone (the brief's decision; 3 messages left in the burst, from the size-changed reconfigure); a third change to the vendored wgpu-core that re-queries the surface just before `vkCreateSwapchainKHR` and creates the swapchain at its fixed extent (from the triage update; prototyped in #409, it closed the race, 0 messages in 8 burst runs with `bevy_render` unchanged, but `configure` would create a size other than the one asked for while the `wgpu` front end, not vendored, keeps describing each `SurfaceTexture` with the requested size, on every resize without the Bevy change and in the race with it; a change to wgpu's contract with no upstream counterpart, so no drop condition and a re-port on every wgpu major); that change with the `wgpu` front end vendored too and told the size used (consistent sizes, but a new wgpu-core API and two forked crates, the same objection); present scaling through `VK_EXT_swapchain_maintenance1` (the VUID doesn't apply with a `VkSwapchainPresentScalingCreateInfoEXT` in the chain, and wgpu's contract stays; unsupported by wgpu-hal 29.0.4, not prototyped, and a frame caught by the race would be shown scaled); keeping R-16 at zero for resizes with a follow-up issue on present scaling (not chosen) | `gpu_window_resized_seconds_apart_leaves_no_validation_message` (fails before the change: one `07781` per resize) and `gpu_window_resized_in_a_burst_settles_with_no_message_but_the_resize_race` (any message other than `07781` fails it; the `07781` are counted) in `crates/ftgsxr-desktop/tests/window_resize.rs`; the size-changed site has no test that fails without it, since the stale size it fixes gives the race's message and depends on when the window system's events arrive (in the burst, 9 messages over 6 resizes before the change, 3 in a 7-resize burst with the `Outdated` site alone, 0 or 1 with both), each running itself again as a child and reading the layer's own log (Q-113); | Q-325 | §1.12 (72 Hz), §1.4, D-30, Dv-2, Q-138, Q-239 | nothing says when the adapter compiles its splat passes; `prepare_view` compiled them at the first view's prepare, synchronously on the render thread: 321 ms on the Quest 3, inside Dv-2's window (frame 1 at +0.31 s after Running, about 23 frames), and again the first time the governor switches the sort mode | once per device and tier (`Views` reset, Q-239), `prepare_views` starts compiling the `LinearOver` passes of both sort modes, keys (Rgba16Float, per view) and (Rgba16Float, shared center), each on a thread of its own (not the async compute pool, where model loads could hold it queued); a cache miss on one of those keys joins its thread, waiting when it is not done, so a view's first frame still draws; other keys (`FileEncoded`) compile as before | the passes are made by the same `SplatPasses::new` with the same tier, so the renders are unchanged; on the Quest the warm-up starts at the session's Available state, 1.0 s before Running, and has finished when the eyes take it (frame 1 at +0.011 s; five Dv-2 runs in a row 701–709 frames, 70.1–70.9 Hz, against 690–691 before M-5 and 477–541 on `main`); the desktop rig draws `LinearOver` at per view too, and the governor may switch either rig to shared center | compile synchronously at device creation (blocks the main app's startup 0.3 s); warm only the key of an existing splat camera (the eyes become splat cameras only once located, at Running, too late); draw nothing until the task is ready (a view's first frames lose their splats); widen Dv-2's window instead (Q-138, the owner's) | Dv-2 (frames in the window's first second); the `gpu_` adapter tests, whose first frames now take the warm-up's passes | confirmed |
| Q-326 | §1.10, §1.6 Audio ("audio position = (τ_m − τ₀,m)/r_m"), Q-149, Q-187 | Q-187's `position -> Option<f64>` is `None` only for an unknown sink; nothing says what the port does once a soundtrack plays to its end and kira stops and drops it (a loop's wrap after a soundtrack as long as the span, #538) | `AudioOutput::position` is `None` for a sink that played to its end and stopped, until a `seek`; a `seek` on such a sink plays it again, paused, at the position, keeping its gain and pan; a bare `resume` on a stopped sink without a seek first does nothing. `StubAudioOutput` does the same (its `sinks()` shows such a sink's last position while `position()` reads `None`); `follow_clock` always seeks before it resumes, and does not revive a sink while p is within `DRIFT_S` of the soundtrack's end (no tail replay before the wrap). Signatures unchanged | kira stops a sound at its end and its handle keeps the last position, so the adapter must say it stopped; reviving only on a seek keeps the position (τ_m − τ₀,m)/r_m for any caller, where a bare resume would play from an unstated position | `None` kept as unknown-only, with a `stopped()` query on the port (a signature change); a bare resume playing from 0 (the first fix, which breaks §1.6's position for a direct caller) | `kira_output_plays_a_sink_again_after_its_end`, `stub_resume_without_a_seek_leaves_a_stopped_sink_stopped` | confirmed |
| Q-327 | §1.8 Hands, Appendix D.3, Appendix E.1 (its extension list), D-25, Q-262 | Q-262's revised rule (#540) gives a held controller priority, but nothing says how the player knows a controller is held: a controller set down on a table stays tracked and active on `/user/hand/<side>`, and would keep the hand from ever owning that side | with `XrPlugin.hands`, XR\_META\_detached\_controllers is enabled beside §1.8's hand extensions (absent: one E-XR-UNSUPPORTED warning like theirs); `tracking` then creates a pose action `detached_grip` with the subaction paths `/user/detached_controller_meta/{left,right}`, bound to their `input/grip/pose` in every suggested profile and never read, so the runtime's in-hand detection has a profile to move a set-down controller to, off `/user/hand/<side>`. "Held" is the aim action active on `/user/hand/<side>` (Q-128's test) with no profile current on `/user/detached_controller_meta/<side>` (`xrGetCurrentInteractionProfile`, read once a frame) and, with XR\_EXT\_hand\_tracking and XR\_FB\_hand\_tracking\_aim enabled, a profile other than `khr/simple_controller` current on `/user/hand/<side>` (owner, revision 33, #548: a controller asleep or without its battery never reaches the detached path, while the tracked hand drives `khr/simple_controller` on the hand path and keeps the aim active, so the side stayed the controller's and its model was drawn over the hand); without the detached extension the detached path reads none, and without the hand extensions every profile counts, so on a runtime with neither an active controller counts as held. Known limit (accepted, revision 33, #548): a real controller that a runtime maps to `khr/simple_controller` while the hand extensions are enabled is not held; on the Quest 3 a real controller's profile is a Touch one, which the player always suggests. Dv-8 reports whether the extension is enabled, and `tracking` logs each side's current interaction profile on the hand and detached paths at the app's first frame and when either changes (a failed read logged as an error and counted as no profile). Measured on the Quest 3 (owner, 2026-11-05, A-11 by hand): a set-down controller's `meta/touch_controller_plus` moves to the detached path while the tracked hand drives `khr/simple_controller` on the hand path, so the aim alone stayed active and the side the controller's, its model drawn and the hand never shown; hence the detached-path test. A detached controller with an untracked or unavailable hand leaves the side with no owner (accepted by the owner, 2026-11-05: a put-down controller is not in use, so the side waits for the hand); and Dv-8's pulse clause needs a held controller, since a set-down one drops out of `XrHands` | the owner's direction (#540) prefers a built-in signal over geometry, and the pinned openxr 0.21.1 carries this one; the extension makes a given profile current on at most one of the paired hand and detached paths, and defines in-hand detection as the runtime's (source-log 315); binding on the detached paths only when the extension is enabled keeps their paths away from a runtime that would reject them (Monado) | the owner's geometric test from `tracking`'s data: a controller held while its grip lies within about 10 to 15 cm of the same side's wrist joint, with a larger release radius (hysteresis), thresholds measured on the device, and an orientation check added only if distance misfires; the interaction-profile-changed event rather than a read every frame; a Touch profile current on the hand path as the test (equivalent on the Quest 3, but it ties "held" to the profile list); the aim action alone (failed on the Quest 3, #540) | A-11 (with the controllers set down, a hand in view shows its tracked ray) with the `interaction profile` log lines (with a controller set down, as measured: `hand /interaction_profiles/khr/simple_controller, detached /interaction_profiles/meta/touch_controller_plus`, the controller model hidden and the hand drawn); Dv-8's `detached controllers` line; `extensions_ask_for_the_hand_extensions_only_with_hands`, `missing_hand_extensions_names_each_absent_one`; `controller_held_needs_the_aim_active_and_nothing_detached`; `controller_held_rejects_a_hand_driven_simple_controller` (#548); #548's A-11 check with one controller asleep (its side `hand /interaction_profiles/khr/simple_controller, detached none`, the hand drawn); the M-3 controller test and `xr_hands_requested_on_monado_keeps_controllers_and_logs_no_error` unchanged otherwise | in spec (D-55) |

| Q-332 | §1.8 User interface ("`select` clicks, holding `select` drags"; the wrist menu "uses the same pointer"), U-37, I-11, Q-263, Q-265, Q-267, Q-290, Q-291 | The spec doesn't say how far the hit point may move between a press and its release and still click; egui's default click tolerance, 6 points, is about 2.6 mm on the wrist menu's 0.16 m quad at the panel zoom, less than a quick click moves on a menu that rides a tracked hand, so the button flashes and its action doesn't run (from #558: panel: clicking a left-wrist menu button with the right controller doesn't trigger its action) | the owner (#561): no click tolerance; the wrist menu's buttons behave as on a touch interface (Android, iOS): a `select` press arms the button, which looks pressed while the hit is inside it and not while it is outside; a release with the hit inside clicks it, however far the hit moved and however long the press lasted; a release outside it or off the quad cancels the press. | Q-333 | §1.8 User interface ("`select` clicks, holding `select` drags (sliders, scroll bars)"; the wrist menu "uses the same pointer"), I-11, U-37, Q-263, Q-332 | The spec says `select` clicks but not when a press and release make a click: how far the hit may move, how long the press may last, whether the release must be inside the button, what a button looks like while pressed with the hit outside it, and whether a list scroll cancels the press; egui clicks the widget a press began on when the release comes within 6 points and 0.8 s of the press, wherever the release is, and shows it pressed while `select` is held, wherever the hit is (from #561: panel: a button press should click on any release inside the button, like a touch interface) | on both XR panels, buttons behave as on a touch interface (Android, iOS). A press is *armed* when egui's hit at the press (the widget on or within egui's `interact_radius` of the press) senses clicks and not drags, with no widget that drags under the pointer: the wrist menu's buttons, and on the main panel the tab row, Back and every button and checkbox of the tab's content, since its lists scroll by the stick and their scroll bars, not by dragging their content (egui 0.36 drags content to scroll only on a touch screen). While armed, the widget looks pressed when the hit is inside its rect and not when it is outside; a release with the hit inside clicks it once, however far the hit moved and however long `select` was held; a release outside cancels the press, and so does the hit leaving the quad (where `select`'s release never reaches the panel, Q-263) and a stick scroll during the press (which moves the list under it). A press with a widget that drags under it (a slider, a scroll bar, a list that scrolls by dragging its content) is not armed: it clicks as egui does, so a move past egui's 6 points drags it and cancels the press. On both panels the 0.8 s limit is lifted (`max_click_duration` infinite), so a long still press clicks. `panel_pointer` rewrites each panel context's pointer events for the frame from the context's last pass: while the hit is inside the armed widget egui sees the pointer at the press, so its own click and pressed look hold; a hit back inside after egui gave the click up gives egui a release and a new press at the press point; a cancel, or a release outside whose egui click would still come, is preceded by a move past the tolerance, and leaving the quad also gives egui the release; the pass gives the armed widget its idle look while the hit is outside it and egui still holds the press. The desktop dock under the mouse keeps egui's behaviour | the owner's request (#561), matching the touch behaviour of Android and iOS buttons (from general knowledge of those platforms; no source was read); a hit point on a quad that rides a hand or a held controller moves like a finger, not a mouse, so a distance limit drops slow, wandering presses; the release-inside test replaces the limit, so it depends on neither the quad's size nor `ui_scale`; the rewrite sits in the pointer path only the XR panels use, so the shared tab code and the desktop stay as they are; egui's 6 points stays the slop that starts a scroll or a slider drag, which a larger tolerance would delay | Q-332's distance tolerance, raised on both panels (still drops a slow press, delays drags on the main panel); turn on drag-to-scroll for the panel's lists, so a drag on a list button scrolls the list and cancels the press as on a phone (a change to §1.8's "holding `select` drags (sliders, scroll bars)", and every list button's press would start a scroll past 6 points); keep a press over the quad's edge alive until the hit returns (its release never reaches the panel, so egui's button would stay down); a wrapper around every click widget in the shared tab code (touches the desktop's code); click on press (can't be cancelled); iOS's larger retention area around a pressed button (the owner asked for "inside the button") | the `cpu` tests of #561: on the wrist menu at `ui_scale` 1 and 1.5, a press slid 1.25 cm inside the button, a press held 2 s, and a press slid out and back click once on release, a press slid 2 px clicks once, not twice, a press pressed 3 px outside the button and slid inside clicks once, and one released outside, just over the edge, or taken off the quad and back, doesn't; the button paints pressed only while the hit is inside, and a press taken off the quad isn't left held; on the main panel, a tab press slid 20 px clicks it with one click cue, a list button clicks in place, held 2 s and slid onto another button and back, but not when the stick scrolls during the press, a button in a list that scrolls by dragging doesn't click when the press drags it, and a slider still drags; on the headset, slow wandering presses on the wrist buttons and the main panel's buttons click on a release inside | confirmed |
| Q-334 | §1.8 User interface ("the ray's hit point on the quad is the pointer; `select` clicks, holding `select` drags (sliders, scroll bars); the pointing hand's stick scrolls the focused list"), I-11, Q-263, Q-279, Q-333 | The spec gives each panel one pointer and doesn't say which hand's ray it follows when both hit the panel; egui has one pointer per context, so joining both hands' events makes it jump between them, and the left hand's press lands on the widget under the right hand's ray (from #563: panel: with two rays on the panel, the left hand's pinch clicks nothing) | Each panel context follows one hand, its pointer hand, and only that hand's hit, presses and scroll reach it: a hand holding `select` on the panel keeps the pointer, the other hand's moves, presses and scroll dropped; with no press held, a hand's press on the panel makes it the pointer hand, the context getting its move to its hit then its press (in the same frame as the pointer hand's release, when both come together; when both hands press in one frame, the pointer hand keeps it); with no press held, the last hand to press keeps the pointer while its ray is on the panel, and when it leaves the other's ray takes it with a move and no `PointerGone`; the first hand to reach a panel nobody points at is its pointer hand; the stick scroll follows the pointer hand. The pointer hand's ray leaving during its press ends the press: an armed touch press is cancelled and released as Q-333 says, any other (a slider's drag) gets egui's release at its last hit, then `PointerGone`, and the other ray takes the pointer the next frame, that frame's hit dropped with any press it brings (whose release is then dropped too), so a drag doesn't end at the other's hit; in the frame a press takes the pointer, the former pointer hand's scroll is dropped; a release reaches the context only for a press it got, so a hand that becomes the pointer hand while holding a press that was dropped has its release dropped | egui (0.36) tracks one pointer and one press per context, so one hand must own it; the hand that presses is the one the user means; keeping it through a held press keeps drags and Q-333's touch press intact; egui keeps its button down after `PointerGone`, so without a release the other hand's ray would carry on a slider's drag, and a release in the frame of the other's move would end the drag at the other's hit; a release egui never saw the press of can still count as a click in its pointer state (`could_any_button_be_click` holds on any release within the tolerance) | the right hand always wins (the left can never use the panel while the right points at it, the behaviour before #563); the nearest or most recently moved ray wins (it can switch mid-press and lose the click); egui's multi-touch events (`Event::Touch`) for two pointers (egui's widgets still click with one pointer, and drags would mix); keep a press whose ray left until its hand releases (the release never reaches the panel off the quad) | the `cpu` tests of #563: on the main panel with both rays on it, a left-hand press and release on a tab opens that tab, not the one under the right ray, and the same with the hands swapped; while the left holds `select`, the right's press doesn't reach the panel and the left's release clicks its tab, the right's later release clicking nothing; the pointer hand's events reach the context first on its press, a leaving pointer hand hands over with a move and no `PointerGone`, the other hand's scroll is dropped, and a press whose ray leaves ends with one release; on the headset, each hand's pinch or trigger clicks under its own ray with both on the main panel or the wrist menu | confirmed |

The weight is filtered over the 2 × 2 nearest texels (the owner's answer, #578): E13's weight is computed against each of the four texels whose centres surround the projection, each decoded as above, and the four weights are blended bilinearly by the projection's position among those centres, so the edge of a silhouette is a ramp about one texel wide instead of a step along the texels; a sample is invalid when it is not finite or lies at the far plane (d = 1, no surface in range), and a projection outside the image is outside. | the extension defines the images through an OpenXR projection; nearest sampling keeps every depth a real surface's (Q-171's reason); a far-plane sample means no occluder. | bilinear depth filtering; treating d = 1 as a surface at farZ (hides nothing when far is infinite, so equivalent there). | R-12's synthetic images are written with the same convention, so the private test and A-5 expose a wrong reading; Dv-7 reports the planes. | changed (#601) |

A new public resource of `ftgsxr_app::persist`, `#[derive(Resource, Default)] pub enum SceneAnchor { None, Creating, Ready(String), Locating(String), Lost(String) }`, is the handshake: on `SaveScene` under the `Headset` marker with the effective mode passthrough, `persist` sets `Creating` and defers the write; `anchors` creates and persists an anchor at the content group's origin, sets `Ready(uuid)`, and `persist` then writes the file with `anchor: { uuid }` (a creation that fails or exceeds 2 s writes the file without an anchor and toasts; `NoAnchors`' `create` errs at once, so a headset without the extensions never waits). `audio` sounds the hover on a new hovered entry and the grab sounds on a dragged entry's start and end, and the XR haptics give D-44's hover and grab pulses on the same edges; with one drag the trim is E18's `t₀.drag_handle(handle, δ, ρ)`, with two the first one's result and then the second's own value applied to it (a face's limit through `set_box_limit`, the radius, the center); when a drag of an instance starts or ends, every other drag of it still running is re-anchored, its t₀ becoming the trim the drags last gave that instance (the last `SetTrim` they sent, else the instance's trim) and its p₀ its current drag point, so the trim does not change at the re-anchor, and a new drag's own t₀ is that same trim (owner answer, #655); the gizmo returns to translate whenever a selected instance's trim is off (turned off, reset, or another instance selected), and keeps its mode with nothing selected; the drag's `InstanceAction::SetTrim` is an `Intent::Instance` written after `run_intents` (which reads the intents and so cannot write them) when the shape changes, and applied in the next frame; `hovered` is in hand order (left, right, mouse); pressing the gizmo's current mode again is no change and ends no drag; on the headset a dragging hand is never taken by a panel (its ray, presses and release go to `manipulate`) and its stick does nothing, and a hand's ray is drawn to the trim handle it hovers or drags, at the handle point's projection on the ray, before the instances; a hit on a handle another hand drags is the handles' action and does nothing else (no pick, no grab); t\_h is the hit handle point's projection on the ray; L's eye is the head camera (`head_pose`), else the ray's origin; a dragging hand hovers nothing, and a hand over a handle hovers the handle, not the instance; the handles are drawn as spheres in their own gizmo group with a depth bias of −1 (on top), with the `Headset` marker in any gizmo mode (the desktop's trim-mode condition is E18's, revision 50) | haptics and sounds read edges of components, as `Hovered` and `Grabbed` already do (Q-270); merging keeps both hands' edits while one drag keeps E18 exactly, and re-anchoring keeps them when one hand starts or releases (each drag keeping its own t₀ undid the other hand's edits at a release, #655), at the cost that a face dragged back restores an opposite limit it carried only since the last re-anchor; the projection gives p₀ on the ray where the handle was hit | a message per edge; the last drag winning; each drag keeping its own t₀ through the other's start and release; translate only on a trim turned off; `SetTrim`'s rule applied in place in the drag's frame; a panel taking a dragging hand's ray; a taken handle falling through to the pick; t\_h as E10's box entry distance | `i24_trim_handles` (`TrimHandles`' hover and drag entries, two hands' faces, either hand's release keeping the other's edits (#657), the mode's return), `audio::tests::trim_handle_sounds_follow_the_handles_edges`, `xr_input::system::tests::select_pulses_trim_handles_hover_and_drag`, `trim_handles::tests` | changed (#657) |
| Q-380 | §1.8 User interface (the main panel: "the desktop's tabs (§1.7) without the mouse-only items, plus Environment (§1.9) and Comfort", on its 1024 × 768 texture), §1.7 Panels (D-21), §1.11 `[general] ui_scale`, Q-267, Q-376 | What the main panel does with tab content wider than the panel: the texture fixes the panel's width, about 585 points at `ui_scale` 1 and 390 at 1.5 with Q-267's zoom of 1.75 × `ui_scale`, and the Library tab's rows reach about 385 points (#627); the tab body was a vertical-only scroll area, so content past the panel's right edge was cut off and could not be reached, by touch or by ray (from #649: Q-n candidates: the XR main panel's tab content wider than the panel) | Same rule as Q-376 for the dock: the main panel's tab body, and the sub-pages drawn in its place (the confirmation, the training cameras, the request replay), use the dock's scroll area, which scrolls both ways and is as wide as the panel (no horizontal auto-shrink), so the vertical bar stays at the panel's right edge, inside its frame, and a row wider than the panel is reached by dragging the horizontal bar with a ray. This is the overflow fallback, not the layout: rows are still made to fit the narrowest panel, about 390 points at `ui_scale` 1.5, as #627 made the Library tab's wrap to the tab's width | The two surfaces share their tab bodies (`dock::tab_body`), so one overflow rule keeps them alike and covers later tabs and sections; a control past the texture's edge cannot be used by any input, while a scroll bar can be dragged by a ray.
